# U0823 | Science and technology in news - space missions, defence tests, health and biotech developments
- **Rows:** G-12 - Science and technology in news - space missions, defence tests, health and biotech developments; S4-10 - National - science and technology: space, defence, health and biotechnology developments
- **Exams:** G1-Prelims + G2-Screening | **Tier:** HIGH, 3 pp (~1,350 words) | **PYQs:** APPSC 62, other exams 30, last APPSC year 2025, latest papers 7
- **Keywords:**
  1. *Vaccines, drugs and disease milestones -> developer/manufacturer + country FIRSTs (Covishield, Sputnik V, malaria vaccine, polio-free certification) [I]
  2. *Space missions, satellites and launch vehicles -> agency + objective + target body (Gaganyaan, Chandrayaan, Aditya-L1, NISAR, OSIRIS-APEX, H3) [I]
  3. *Semiconductors, quantum and computing terms -> developer + use (SHAKTI chip, Majorana 1, qubit, GPT) [I]
  4. *Digital initiatives and tech platforms -> launching organisation or department (Digiboxx, Sangam Digital Twin, MeitY initiatives, tech campuses) [I]
  5. *Viruses and diseases in news - pathogen type, outbreak country, WHO declarations (coronavirus family) [I]
  6. *Defence platforms - ships inducted or decommissioned, UAVs, missiles -> service + developer (Agni, BrahMos, Pralay) [I]
  7. *Health emergency and medical projects -> nodal agency (Project BHISHM, Aarogya Maitri Cube, AIIMS conclaves) [I]
  8. *Science Congress and national science events -> edition + venue + theme [I]
  9. +Deep ocean missions and tech FIRSTs -> mission/product + location or maker (Samudrayaan, 3D-printed buildings, observatories, humanoid robots) [I]
  10. *Therapies, vaccines and drugs -> developer + disease + approving regulator (CAR-T cell therapy, gene therapy, DCGI/CDSCO approvals) [II]
  11. *Space missions -> agency + launch vehicle + target (ISRO missions, NASA asteroid missions, foreign rockets) [II]
  12. *Defence developments -> DRDO/service (missiles tested, warships commissioned/decommissioned, aircraft role and generation, indigenous UAVs) [II]
  13. *Technology FIRSTs -> place/state (first digital village, 3D-printed buildings, first indigenous platforms, tech campuses) [II]
  14. *Big-science collaborations and observatories -> partner institutions + location (LIGO-India, Hanle observatories, Indian Biological Data Centre) [II]
  15. *Indigenous health and disaster-response projects -> developing agency + nodal agency (Project BHISHM, Aarogya Maitri cubes) [II]
  16. *Science events - Indian Science Congress session number, venue, theme [II]
  17. +Genome and genetic-medicine projects -> implementing institute + objective (GenomeIndia, monoclonal antibodies, animal cloning) [II]
  18. New technology missions - quantum, semiconductor, AI, green hydrogen -> ministry + outlay head [II]
- **NEW from 2024-25 papers:**
  N1. Blood and immunity terms -> blood groups, antigens and antibodies, universal donor and recipient, Rh factor
- **Latest-paper questions:** G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (NEW: Human biology terms); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (Mission; New developments in S&T); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (Scheme; Initiative); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (Initiative; Digital technologies); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (Rockets; Satellite); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (Defence); G1 Prelims 2024 P2 (Vaccine; First)

### INS Surya — first indigenous Fleet Support Ship launched at HSL
## 1. Launch
- Launched by: Defence Minister Rajnath Singh, 6 October 2026, Hindustan Shipyard Ltd (HSL), Visakhapatnam
- Surya (FSS-1): India's first indigenously designed and built Fleet Support Ship; launched into Bay of Bengal
- Also: keel of FSS-5 laid; upgraded Slipway-4 inaugurated; 300-tonne Goliath Crane commissioned
## 2. Features
- At-sea replenishment; cargo and fluids; first-line maintenance workshops; helicopter deck for Multi-Role Helicopter; self-defence systems
- Slipway-4: ships up to 45,000 DWT
- Goliath Crane: spans Slipways 3 and 4; made by L&T Geostructure with Konecranes (Make in India); block-construction
## 3. Background — HSL
- Under Ministry of Defence (⚠ not Ports & Shipping); Miniratna Category-I in 2025
- Jala Usha — first ship built in independent India, launched at Visakhapatnam in 1948
- Vizag hub: Eastern Naval Command, Naval Dockyard, HSL, Visakhapatnam Port
## 4. More details
- Built and launched in just 18 months; HSL building five FSS for the Navy; each ~43,500 tonnes displacement
- HSL established 1941
### Jaya AUV — CSIR-NIO and HSL
## 1. Key facts
- Jaya: indigenous Autonomous Underwater Vehicle by CSIR-NIO, Goa (est. 1966) with Navy design wing
- HSL Visakhapatnam signed ToT for mass production — HSL's entry into hi-tech marine platforms
- 54 kg, 1.7 m, depth up to 200 m; ~80% Indian components; modular; CTD sensor, side-scan sonar
- ⚠ AUV autonomous vs ROV remotely operated
## 2. Collaborator
- Navy partner named as the Indian Navy's Design Directorate
### National Supercomputing Mission — 40 supercomputers, 68 PF
## 1. Status (September 2026)
- 40 supercomputers, combined 68 petaFLOPS (⚠ target 50 systems, >123 PF)
- 13 high-end (>1 PF); 12 mid-range (500 TF–1 PF); 15 below 500 TF
- Rudra servers: ~6,000 deployed, 1,500 under production
## 2. About NSM
| Item | Detail |
| Launched | April 2015 |
| Outlay | ~₹4,500 crore |
| Steered by | DST and MeitY |
| Implemented by | C-DAC Pune and IISc Bengaluru |
| Build approach | Assembly → Manufacturing → Design & manufacturing |
| Backbone | National Knowledge Network (⚠ does not make servers — C-DAC designed Rudra) |
## 3. Milestones and indigenous tech
- PARAM 8000 (C-DAC, 1991) — 1 gigaflop; PARAM Yuva — 54 teraFLOPS
- PARAM Rudra — indigenous Rudra servers + Indian software stack
- PARAM Shavak — "supercomputing-in-a-box" for colleges
- Indigenous interconnect 100/200 Gbps; indigenous cooling; HPC Shiksha Portal; EduHPC; FDPs with AICTE
- Applications: flood early warning (Mahanadi basin, 2 days ahead); forest-fire model (Sikkim Himalayas); Urban Environment DSS; seismic imaging; COVID drug screening
- Units: tera = trillion, peta = quadrillion, exa = quintillion FLOPS; world's fastest (2026) ~2.19 exaFLOPS; India generates ~20% of world's data
## 4. ESTIC-2026
- Emerging Science, Technology and Innovation Conclave — 2nd edition, 27–29 October 2026, Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi
### Nuclear power — ₹7.5 lakh crore potential private investment
## 1. Key facts
- AEC Chairman Ajit Kumar Mohanty — target 100 GW nuclear by 2047; private investment ~₹7.5 lakh crore for ~30 GW; ~₹25 crore per MW
- NPCIL: 24 reactors (~9 GW); 17 under construction (13 GW); est. 1987, Mumbai
- India ~25% of world thorium; SMRs (up to 300 MW) for steel, cement
## 2. Background
- Three-stage programme (Homi Bhabha): PHWR (natural U) → FBR (Pu) → U-233 from Th-232; thorium in monazite sands of Kerala, TN, Odisha, AP
### Nuclear power in space — RTGs, fission and governance
## 1. Systems
| System | How it works | Examples / use |
| RTG (Radioisotope Thermoelectric Generator) | Heat from natural decay of plutonium-238 → electricity; no moving parts | Voyager 1 & 2, Curiosity, Perseverance |
| RHU (Radioisotope Heater Unit) | Heat only — ⚠ no electricity | Keeps instruments from freezing |
| Fission reactor | Controlled chain reaction → much higher power | Lunar/Martian habitats |
| Nuclear Thermal Propulsion (NTP) | Reactor heats liquid hydrogen propellant | ~twice efficiency of chemical rockets |
| Nuclear Electric Propulsion (NEP) | Electricity accelerates ionised propellant | Low thrust, very high efficiency |
- Radioisotope systems used since 1961; Voyager launched 1977 (5-year mission), now >20 billion km away
## 2. Plans
- India: ISRO invited proposals (2021) for a 100-watt RTG; NASA invited ISRO to its Moon Base programme
- NASA Dragonfly to Titan (Saturn) — launch July 2028, radioisotope generator
- NASA (Aug 2025): lunar fission system ≥100 kW by Q1 FY2030
- White House memo (Apr 2026): 20-kW reactors in orbit by 2028; on Moon by 2030
- China & Russia: reactor on Moon by mid-2030s for International Lunar Research Station
## 3. Legal framework
| Instrument | Key point |
| Outer Space Treaty 1967 | Bans nuclear weapons/WMD in orbit — ⚠ does not ban peaceful nuclear power |
| Liability Convention 1972 | Launching State absolutely liable for damage on Earth / to aircraft in flight |
| UN Principles on NPS 1992 | Safety, radiation protection, re-entry notification — ⚠ only non-propulsive use |
| COPUOS–IAEA Safety Framework 2009 | Guidance — not binding |
- Cosmos 954: Soviet satellite re-entered over northern Canada (1978); settled 1981 for C$3 million
- Pu-238 shortage: US restarted production in 2015 after ~3 decades

## 1. Key facts

- WHO 90–70–90 by 2030: 90% girls vaccinated by 15; 70% women screened at 35 & 45; 90% treated; WHO Global Strategy 2020

### Additive manufacturing — NSAM progress and NSAM 2.0
## 1. National Strategy for Additive Manufacturing (NSAM)
- Released by: MeitY in February 2022 (⚠ not Ministry of Heavy Industries)
- Targets: 5% of global AM market; ~USD 1 billion to GDP; 50 India-specific technologies; 100 start-ups; 500 products; train 1 lakh workers; 10 sectors
- 7 Development and Deployment Centres
- Progress: >1.54 lakh trained; 56 start-ups; 65 India-specific technologies
- NSAM 2.0: commercialisation, IPR, digital integration, affordable machines/materials, defence, AM-electronics
- 2nd National Additive Manufacturing Symposium: MeitY, 13 March 2026, New Delhi
## 2. Concepts
- AM = 3D printing — layer by layer; >40 processes
- STL (Standard Tessellation Language) file — surface as triangles; slicing software makes layers
- Materials: ABS, polycarbonate, PLA, PVA (water-soluble supports); titanium etc.; zirconia, alumina
## 3. Applications in news
- Agnikul Cosmos (IIT Madras incubated) — Agnibaan SOrTeD, May 2024 — single-piece 3D-printed semi-cryogenic engine
- Indian Army — first 3D-printed two-storey dwelling at Ahmedabad in 12 weeks
- Kelvin 6K Pro printer — 2,500 sq ft house in <30 days (geopolymer); ~30% lower cost, up to 90% lower emissions
- Global AM machines market USD 149 billion by 2035; AM electronics USD 18 → 112 billion
- Atal Tinkering Labs: Classes VI–XII; grant ₹20 lakh; 1,200–1,500 sq ft

### EOS-05 on GSLV-F17 — first imaging satellite from geosynchronous orbit
## 1. Mission
- Launched: 4 September 2026, GSLV-F17, Second Launch Pad, SDSC-SHAR Sriharikota
- ⚠ India's first imaging satellite from geosynchronous orbit
- Mass: ~2,367 kg; placed in Sub-GTO (perigee 170 km, apogee 28,934 km, inclination 19.28°, azimuth 104°) — not directly in GEO
## 2. GSLV-F17
- 19th GSLV flight; height 51.7 m; lift-off mass 420.5 t; 3 stages; 4 m ogive fairing
| Stage | Detail |
| GS1 | S139 solid core + 4 L40H liquid strap-ons |
| GS2 | GL40HT liquid stage |
| GS3 | CUS15 cryogenic (LH2 + LOX) |
- GSLV Mk II capacity: ~2,250 kg to GTO, 6,000 kg to LEO; first flight 18 April 2001; indigenous cryo stage from GSLV-D5 (January 2014)
## 3. Orbits background
- LEO 160–2,000 km (orbit 90–120 min) — ~15,562 active payloads (Sept 4, 2026); India has 22 operational LEO satellites (April 2026 ISRO report)
- Geosynchronous period ~23 h 56 min — may be elliptical/inclined; geostationary — circular, equatorial, zero inclination, ~35,786 km; India has 31 GEO satellites
- Galileo (Europe): 30 satellites in MEO
- CA-360: EOS-05 also called GISAT-1A; images subcontinent every 30 min (priority areas ~5 min) from ~36,000 km
## 4. CA-360 extras
- Uses: agriculture, land resources, environmental and weather monitoring, disaster management
- ⚠ Unlike LEO/Sun-Synchronous EO satellites, it watches the same large area with very high temporal frequency
- Geostationary orbit: ~35,786 km above the equator; period ~24 hours; appears stationary — used for communications, weather, continuous regional monitoring
- SDSC: Sriharikota, AP — ISRO's principal launch centre; former name Sriharikota Range; named after Prof. Satish Dhawan
### ISRO semi-cryogenic Power Head Test Article at full thrust
## 1. Test
- Hot test of Semi-Cryogenic Engine Power Head Test Article (PHTA) at 100% rated level = 200 tonnes thrust
- Place: ISRO Propulsion Research Complex (IPRC), Mahendragiri, Tamil Nadu
- 9th hot test in series; ⚠ first at full rated level
- ⚠ Total duration 35 seconds, but only 5 seconds at full level
- Validated switch from low-pressure start tank to medium-pressure run tank
- ⚠ PHTA includes all engine systems except the thrust chamber
| Level | Thrust |
| 47% | 94 t |
| 100% | 200 t |
## 2. Background
- SE2000 — 2,000 kN-class engine powering the SC120 semi-cryogenic stage
- SC120 to replace L110 core stage of LVM3; with uprated C32 cryogenic upper stage, raises LVM3 payload

### 51st Doppler Weather Radar at Sambalpur and Mission Mausam
## 1. News

- 40 more radars planned under Mission Mausam
| Band | Use |
| X-band | Localised thunderstorms, lightning |
| C-band | Rainfall, cyclone tracking |
| S-band | Deep coastal, long-range cyclone monitoring |
- Uses radio waves (not sound); updates ~every 10 minutes; range ~500 km; Doppler effect — Christian Doppler (Austrian)
## 2. Mission Mausam
- Approved: September 2024, ₹2,000 crore for 2 years; launched: January 14, 2025 at IMD's 150th foundation day
- Goal: "weather-ready" and "climate-smart" nation; implemented by IMD, IITM, NCMRWF (Ministry of Earth Sciences)

- ⚠ CA-360 gives inauguration as September 16, 2026 (daily: September 17); range — ⚠ 250-km radius (CA-360) vs ~500 km (daily)
- Mission Mausam plans 40–50 more radars — network of ~100 within two years
- Mission Mausam (CA-360): approved 2024, ₹2,000 crore, Ministry of Earth Sciences
### Oceansat-3 (EOS-06) tracks El Niño impact
## 1. Findings
- EOS-06/Oceansat-3 detected weaker easterly winds (westerly reversals) and lower chlorophyll-a in June 2026 — developing El Niño in tropical Pacific
- Analysis by NRSC (ISRO) of April–June data, 2023–2026; productive zone contracted westward
- Combined OCM-3 chlorophyll data with scatterometer winds
## 2. Background
- Launched: November 26, 2022, PSLV-C54, Sriharikota — polar sun-synchronous orbit; 3rd-generation Oceansat
- Payloads: Ocean Colour Monitor-3, Ku-band Scatterometer (SCAT-3), Sea Surface Temperature Monitor, ARGOS
- ⚠ El Niño weakens upwelling → lower phytoplankton

### Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope
## 1. Key facts
- Launched: August 30, 2026 by NASA on a SpaceX rocket
- Type: Wide-field infrared telescope; orbit Sun–Earth L2 (~1.2 million km)
- Mass: ~8,000 kg; mission: 5 years; 1.4 TB/day; up to 20 petabytes total
- Instruments: Wide Field Instrument (300-megapixel infrared camera; field 1.5× full Moon) and Coronagraph
- Processes images ~1,000 times faster than Hubble
- Ground stations: New Mexico, Australia, Japan
- Named after: Nancy Grace Roman — NASA's first Chief Astronomer, "Mother of Hubble"
## 2. Background
- Universe: ordinary matter ~5%, dark matter ~27%, dark energy ~68%
- Hubble: since 1990; JWST: launched 2021, also at L2; 6,000+ exoplanets known
### BAM-H24 rocket propellant patent
## 1. News
- BAM-H24 — high-energy rocket-propellant material — got an Indian patent
- Developed by DRDO and University of Hyderabad's Advanced Centre of Research in High Energy Materials (ACRHEM)
- Boron-, hydrogen- and nitrogen-rich compound; high chemical and thermal stability; resists friction — safer than conventional high-energy materials
- High specific impulse and low ignition delay — better solid rocket propellant performance; low moisture absorption — storage stability
## 2. Background
- Specific impulse: key measure of rocket-engine efficiency — higher = better propulsion per unit propellant
- Solid propellant: fuel + oxidiser in a solid mixture — long storage, rapid readiness
- DRDO: est. 1958; under Department of Defence Research and Development, Ministry of Defence
### Agnikul Cosmos rocket component recycling facilities, Chennai
## 1. News
- IIT Madras-incubated Agnikul Cosmos opened facilities at its Chennai research campus to recover, recycle, test and reuse components of launched rockets
- Stage Testing and Inspection Facility (STIF) — tests recovered components
- Pressurant Tank Manufacturing, Overwrapping and Integration Facility — high-pressure propellant/pressurant tanks
- IIT Madras: reuse could cut rocket manufacturing cost by up to 80%
## 2. Background
- Agnikul Cosmos: est. 2017; small-satellite launcher Agnibaan
- Semi-cryogenic engine: refined hydrocarbon fuel + liquid oxygen; cryogenic: liquid hydrogen + liquid oxygen
- Reusable launch vehicles recover and reuse major components instead of building a new vehicle each launch

### Elias 2-24 b — youngest known planet
## 1. News
- Confirmed on September 17, 2026 — age < 1 million years
- Gas giant, Jupiter-like mass; orbits young star Elias 2-24, ~450 light-years away
- Sits in a gap of the protoplanetary disk, still accreting gas and dust — earliest stage of planet formation
- Protoplanetary disk: gas, dust, debris around a young star where planets form
### Uttarakhand's first test-tube Badri calf
## 1. News
- ICAR–National Dairy Research Institute produced Uttarakhand's first Badri female calf by IVF — born September 11, 2026
- Badri: small, hardy indigenous hill cattle of Uttarakhand, adapted to Himalayas — has State Animal status; IVF project since 2023 (crossbreeding threat)
- Oocytes from Badri cows (ultrasound-guided), fertilised with Badri bull semen; embryos put in Sahiwal surrogate cows
## 2. Background

### AlphaGenome Atlas — Google DeepMind
## 1. News
- Launched September 8, 2026 — open-access database for human DNA analysis
- ~1 petabyte of precomputed AI predictions for ~9 billion possible single-letter mutations
- Maps coding and non-coding DNA — non-coding ≈ 98% of the genome
## 2. Background
- Human genome ~3 billion base pairs; single-letter mutations = point mutations / Single-Nucleotide Variants (SNVs)
- Bases A, T, G, C; pairs A–T, G–C; Human Genome Project 1990–2003; 1 PB = 1,000 TB
### DNA strand binding and recombinant cobra antivenom
## 1. DNA strands binding seen directly
- Universities of Sheffield and York (UK) — first direct view of two negatively charged DNA double strands binding, via high-performance Atomic Force Microscopy; question open ~20 years
- Divalent metal ions — nickel, magnesium, calcium — act as atomic bridges
## 2. Recombinant antivenom
- IISc Bengaluru + Technical University of Denmark — protects against Spectacled, Monocled and King Cobra venom
- Lab-made from selected antibodies of camelids (llamas); ⚠ conventional antivenom — venom injected into horses
- IISc: 1909, Bengaluru; Institute of Eminence
### Drought-tolerant rice and SPROUT facility
## 1. Foxtail millet gene in rice
- University of Hyderabad plant scientists put a foxtail millet gene into Pusa Basmati (low-water), Sona Masuri and IR-64 — survives El Niño-type drought
- Led by Dr. M. Muthamilarasan (University Millet Lab founder); ₹80 lakh central assistance
- Free seed for field trials in Andhra Pradesh, Karnataka, Maharashtra, Tamil Nadu
- Foxtail millet: Setaria italica; UoH: 1974, Central University
## 2. SPROUT
- Speed Seed Phenotyping and Resource Optimization for Unified Trait Analysis — at BRIC–NIPGR, New Delhi; inaugurated by Dr. Jitendra Singh (September 2026)
- Evaluates plant traits — climate-resilient crops, high-throughput phenotyping
- BRIC–NIPGR: Biotechnology Research and Innovation Council–National Institute of Plant Genome Research; under Department of Biotechnology
### DoT–C-DOT Optical Network Testbed
## 1. News
- DoT and C-DOT signed a 5-year agreement (August 2026) — end-to-end Optical Network Testbed at C-DOT campus, New Delhi, DoT-funded
- Tests, validates, commercialises indigenous optical tech — optical core, optical access, quantum security
- Phase I: 2 years (set-up); Phase II: 3 years (maintenance, support, outreach); also a training platform

| Item | Detail |

## 3. Milestones and indigenous tech
- PARAM 8000 (C-DAC, 1991) — 1 gigaflop; PARAM Yuva — 54 teraFLOPS
- PARAM Rudra — indigenous Rudra servers + Indian software stack

- Applications: flood early warning (Mahanadi basin, 2 days ahead); forest-fire model (Sikkim Himalayas); Urban Environment DSS; seismic imaging; COVID drug screening
- Units: tera = trillion, peta = quadrillion, exa = quintillion FLOPS; world's fastest (2026) ~2.19 exaFLOPS; India generates ~20% of world's data
## 4. ESTIC-2026
- Emerging Science, Technology and Innovation Conclave — 2nd edition, 27–29 October 2026, Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi
### Nuclear power in space — RTGs, fission and governance
## 1. Systems
| System | How it works | Examples / use |
| RTG (Radioisotope Thermoelectric Generator) | Heat from natural decay of plutonium-238 → electricity; no moving parts | Voyager 1 & 2, Curiosity, Perseverance |
| RHU (Radioisotope Heater Unit) | Heat only — ⚠ no electricity | Keeps instruments from freezing |
| Fission reactor | Controlled chain reaction → much higher power | Lunar/Martian habitats |
| Nuclear Thermal Propulsion (NTP) | Reactor heats liquid hydrogen propellant | ~twice efficiency of chemical rockets |
| Nuclear Electric Propulsion (NEP) | Electricity accelerates ionised propellant | Low thrust, very high efficiency |
- Radioisotope systems used since 1961; Voyager launched 1977 (5-year mission), now >20 billion km away
## 2. Plans
- India: ISRO invited proposals (2021) for a 100-watt RTG; NASA invited ISRO to its Moon Base programme
- NASA Dragonfly to Titan (Saturn) — launch July 2028, radioisotope generator
- NASA (Aug 2025): lunar fission system ≥100 kW by Q1 FY2030
- White House memo (Apr 2026): 20-kW reactors in orbit by 2028; on Moon by 2030
- China & Russia: reactor on Moon by mid-2030s for International Lunar Research Station
## 3. Legal framework
| Instrument | Key point |
| Outer Space Treaty 1967 | Bans nuclear weapons/WMD in orbit — ⚠ does not ban peaceful nuclear power |
| Liability Convention 1972 | Launching State absolutely liable for damage on Earth / to aircraft in flight |
| UN Principles on NPS 1992 | Safety, radiation protection, re-entry notification — ⚠ only non-propulsive use |
| COPUOS–IAEA Safety Framework 2009 | Guidance — not binding |
- Cosmos 954: Soviet satellite re-entered over northern Canada (1978); settled 1981 for C$3 million
- Pu-238 shortage: US restarted production in 2015 after ~3 decades

### Additive manufacturing — NSAM progress and NSAM 2.0
## 1. National Strategy for Additive Manufacturing (NSAM)
- Released by: MeitY in February 2022 (⚠ not Ministry of Heavy Industries)
- Targets: 5% of global AM market; ~USD 1 billion to GDP; 50 India-specific technologies; 100 start-ups; 500 products; train 1 lakh workers; 10 sectors
- 7 Development and Deployment Centres
- Progress: >1.54 lakh trained; 56 start-ups; 65 India-specific technologies
- NSAM 2.0: commercialisation, IPR, digital integration, affordable machines/materials, defence, AM-electronics
- 2nd National Additive Manufacturing Symposium: MeitY, 13 March 2026, New Delhi
## 2. Concepts
- AM = 3D printing — layer by layer; >40 processes
- STL (Standard Tessellation Language) file — surface as triangles; slicing software makes layers
- Materials: ABS, polycarbonate, PLA, PVA (water-soluble supports); titanium etc.; zirconia, alumina
## 3. Applications in news
- Agnikul Cosmos (IIT Madras incubated) — Agnibaan SOrTeD, May 2024 — single-piece 3D-printed semi-cryogenic engine
- Indian Army — first 3D-printed two-storey dwelling at Ahmedabad in 12 weeks
- Kelvin 6K Pro printer — 2,500 sq ft house in <30 days (geopolymer); ~30% lower cost, up to 90% lower emissions
- Global AM machines market USD 149 billion by 2035; AM electronics USD 18 → 112 billion
- Atal Tinkering Labs: Classes VI–XII; grant ₹20 lakh; 1,200–1,500 sq ft

### EOS-05 on GSLV-F17 — first imaging satellite from geosynchronous orbit
## 1. Mission
- Launched: 4 September 2026, GSLV-F17, Second Launch Pad, SDSC-SHAR Sriharikota
- ⚠ India's first imaging satellite from geosynchronous orbit
- Mass: ~2,367 kg; placed in Sub-GTO (perigee 170 km, apogee 28,934 km, inclination 19.28°, azimuth 104°) — not directly in GEO
## 2. GSLV-F17
- 19th GSLV flight; height 51.7 m; lift-off mass 420.5 t; 3 stages; 4 m ogive fairing
| Stage | Detail |
| GS1 | S139 solid core + 4 L40H liquid strap-ons |
| GS2 | GL40HT liquid stage |
| GS3 | CUS15 cryogenic (LH2 + LOX) |
- GSLV Mk II capacity: ~2,250 kg to GTO, 6,000 kg to LEO; first flight 18 April 2001; indigenous cryo stage from GSLV-D5 (January 2014)
## 3. Orbits background
- LEO 160–2,000 km (orbit 90–120 min) — ~15,562 active payloads (Sept 4, 2026); India has 22 operational LEO satellites (April 2026 ISRO report)
- Geosynchronous period ~23 h 56 min — may be elliptical/inclined; geostationary — circular, equatorial, zero inclination, ~35,786 km; India has 31 GEO satellites
- Galileo (Europe): 30 satellites in MEO
- CA-360: EOS-05 also called GISAT-1A; images subcontinent every 30 min (priority areas ~5 min) from ~36,000 km
## 4. CA-360 extras
- Uses: agriculture, land resources, environmental and weather monitoring, disaster management
- ⚠ Unlike LEO/Sun-Synchronous EO satellites, it watches the same large area with very high temporal frequency
- Geostationary orbit: ~35,786 km above the equator; period ~24 hours; appears stationary — used for communications, weather, continuous regional monitoring
- SDSC: Sriharikota, AP — ISRO's principal launch centre; former name Sriharikota Range; named after Prof. Satish Dhawan

### Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope
## 1. Key facts
- Launched: August 30, 2026 by NASA on a SpaceX rocket
- Type: Wide-field infrared telescope; orbit Sun–Earth L2 (~1.2 million km)
- Mass: ~8,000 kg; mission: 5 years; 1.4 TB/day; up to 20 petabytes total
- Instruments: Wide Field Instrument (300-megapixel infrared camera; field 1.5× full Moon) and Coronagraph
- Processes images ~1,000 times faster than Hubble
- Ground stations: New Mexico, Australia, Japan
- Named after: Nancy Grace Roman — NASA's first Chief Astronomer, "Mother of Hubble"
## 2. Background
- Universe: ordinary matter ~5%, dark matter ~27%, dark energy ~68%
- Hubble: since 1990; JWST: launched 2021, also at L2; 6,000+ exoplanets known

### India's space sovereignty: reforms and missions
## 1. Ecosystem
- Space start-ups: 1 (2014) to ~440 (2026)
- Space economy: USD 9 billion (Aug 2026); projected USD 40–45 billion in a decade
- Private investment: USD 100.5 million (FY22) to USD 618.5 million (Mar 2026)
- EO-PPP (IN-SPACe): ⚠ India's first privately owned Earth observation constellation; ~₹1,200 crore private commitment
- NSIL revenue: ₹321.77 crore (FY22) to ₹3,000+ crore (FY25)
- Foreign satellites launched: 35 before 2014 to 399 (Jan 2026); 300+ agreements with 61 countries
## 2. Reforms
- Sector opened to private players 2020; Indian Space Policy 2023
- FDI (Feb 2024): 74% automatic — satellite manufacturing/operations; 49% — launch vehicles, spaceports; 100% — components
- NGP 2024; ₹1,000 crore VC Fund; ₹500 crore Technology Adoption Fund; ₹75 crore Satellite Bus as a Service
## 3. Capabilities
- Launch vehicles: PSLV, GSLV, GSLV Mk III, SSLV — 12 missions July 2023–June 2026
- Sriharikota: 2 pads, third coming; Kulasekarapattinam (Tamil Nadu): second launch site (SSLV, private)
- Chandrayaan-3 (2023): first near south pole; 4th to soft-land
- Mars Orbiter: orbit 24 September 2014; XPoSat: January 2024
- SPADEX: January 2025 — India 4th nation to dock autonomously
- NavIC: coverage up to 1,500 km beyond India
## 4. Future missions
| Mission | Detail |
| Gaganyaan | Approved Jan 2019; expanded Sept 2024 to ₹20,193 crore, 8 missions; 3 astronauts, 400 km, 3 days; uncrewed Q4 2026, crewed 2027 |
| Bharatiya Antariksh Station | 5 modules; BAS-01 by 2028; full by 2035 |
| Chandrayaan-4 | Sample return, 2027 |
| Chandrayaan-5/LUPEX | With JAXA |
| Venus Orbiter Mission | March 2028 |
- Tech: 200-tonne semi-cryogenic engine; NGLV with LOX-methane
## 5. Gaganyaan thermal shield and systems
- Ablative materials: carbon phenolic, silica phenolic — single use
- HLVM3: ~53 m, ~640 t, 3 stages, ~10 t to LEO
- Orbital Module: pressurised Crew Module + unpressurised Service Module (separates before re-entry)
- Parachutes: 10 of 4 types (apex cover, drogue, pilot, main); sea splashdown
- Milestones: SRE-1 (2007); LVM3-X/CARE (2014); Pad Abort Test (2018); TV-D1; Integrated Air Drop Tests
- ⚠ Re-entry heat comes mainly from compression of air, not friction
## 6. Private space updates (CA-360)
- EO constellation: IN-SPACe signed for India's first fully commercial EO constellation (PPP) — Allied Orbits consortium led by PixxelSpace, with Dhruva Space, PierSight, SatSure; 12 satellites (panchromatic, multispectral, hyperspectral, SAR); ₹1,200 crore+; by 2029 (Dr Jitendra Singh in Lok Sabha)
- PowerBank-50: ⚠ India's first satellite battery with "Flight Heritage" — TakeMe2Space (Hyderabad); flew on Skyroot's Vikram-1 "Mission Aagaman", 18 July 2026; 50+ Wh, 380 g
- Powered Cosmoserve Space's payload at 450 km; designed for LEO (160–2,000 km) life of up to 5 years

### Indigenous propulsion: Rotating Detonation Engine and 350-kg turbojet
## 1. RDE
- ⚠ India's first indigenous 5-kN air-breathing Rotating Detonation Engine with aerospike nozzle — D-Propulse Aerospace, at a DRDO facility in Hyderabad; reached TRL-5
- TRL scale: 1 (basic principles) to 9 (proven in operations); TRL-5 = validated in relevant environment
## 2. Turbojet
- 350-kg thrust expendable turbojet for cruise missiles, UAVs, drones — designed by GTRE Bengaluru, built by Azad Engineering (Hyderabad) within 24 months
- 350-kgf expendable turbojet — GTRE (DRDO); first indigenous, for missiles and UAVs (Jul 2026)
### AK-203 "Sher" — first fully indigenised rifle
## 1. Test
- ⚠ First 100% indigenised AK-203 prototype "Sher" — tested by Indo-Russian Rifles Pvt Ltd (India–Russia JV) at Korwa Ordnance facility, Amethi (UP)
- Calibre: 7.62×39 mm; made with Indian components and raw materials
### AI fighter and loitering munition firsts
## 1. Firsts
- FWD Supreme Light: Flying Wedge Defence and Aerospace — billed as ⚠ India's first AI-piloted fighter-aircraft programme; physical demonstrator unveiled August 2026
- Proposed: endurance up to 30 hours, range ~3,000 km, demonstrator ~250 kg; ISR, swarm, precision strike
- Divyastra Mk3: jet-powered loitering munition by Lucknow's Kawa UAV / HoverIt — ⚠ India's first flight of a jet-powered loitering munition with fully indigenous propulsion
- Loitering munition: "kamikaze" drone destroyed with its warhead on impact
- Vahak-6: ⚠ first indigenous flying drone carrier — EndureAir with BEL (est. 1954); mothership Sabal-20 releases Alakh micro-drones

### Generative biology: AI-designed bacteriophages
## 1. Experiment
- Who: Stanford University and Arc Institute; models Evo 1 and Evo 2 (genome language models)
- What: AI designed complete genomes of ΦX174-like bacteriophages; 285 designs tested in E. coli, 16 functional — some beat bacterial resistance
- ⚠ AI proposed blueprints; humans synthesised and tested — no human virus made
- ΦX174: first complete DNA genome sequenced (1977)
| Stage | Period |
| Reading genomes | 1970s |
| Writing (synthesis) | 2000s |
| Modifying (editing, gain-of-function) | 2010s |
- Uses: phage therapy against AMR, AAV gene-delivery vectors, oncolytic viruses
- Risk: capability amplification; need functional DNA screening (what a sequence does, not only what it resembles)

### Vikram-1 — India's first private orbital launch
## 1. What happened
- Stacking (early July): first stage of Vikram-I stacked at First Launch Pad, SDSC Sriharikota — ⚠ first privately built orbital-class rocket stacked there
- Launch: 18 July 2026, 12:05:30 PM, SDSC Sriharikota — ⚠ India's first private orbital rocket launch from Indian soil
- Developer: Skyroot Aerospace, Hyderabad
- Mission name: Mission Aagaman
- Orbit: about 450 km LEO, payload injected about 15 minutes after lift-off
- ⚠ India became third country after USA and China with private orbital launch capability
## 2. Vehicle
| Feature | Detail |
| Stages | 4 — 3 solid + liquid upper stage (orbital adjustment module) |
| Upper-stage engine | RAMAN-I — 3D-printed liquid engine (tested at LPSC) |
| Payload | up to 350 kg to LEO |
## 3. Payloads
| Payload | Purpose |
| SCOPE | Skyroot's own satellite — flight data |
| Grahaa / SOLARAS S3 | Earth observation / tech |
| DCUBED | German tech demonstration |
| Cosmoserve Embrace | Robotic arm — debris capture |
| Cosmic Bloom | Art payload |
| 18-karat gold micro-rocket | Tribute to Indian scientists |
## 4. Background
- Vikram-S (2022): ⚠ sub-orbital — first private Indian rocket to reach space; Vikram-1 is orbital
- Agnikul Cosmos (2024): launched from India's first private launch pad Dhanush
- SSLV ToT to HAL: February 2026
- Indian Space Policy 2023; sector opened 2020
- IN-SPACe (June 2026): 4,500+ organisations registered, 133 authorisations, 106 MoUs
- NSIL (July 2026): 141 satellites launched (138 customer, 3 Indian)
- Funds: IN-SPACe Seed Fund; ₹1,000 crore VC Fund; ₹500 crore Technology Adoption Fund (up to 60% of project cost)
- LEO: 160–2,000 km; orbit period 90–120 min
- Second spaceport: Kulasekarapattinam, Tamil Nadu; Sriharikota near Pulicat Lake
## 5. More details (CA360)
- Three solid stages + liquid Orbital Adjustment Module (OAM); diameter about 1.7 m (⚠ CA360 gives height about 23 m; daily source 22 m)
- Firsts: all-carbon composite structure; first 3D-printed liquid engine in an Indian rocket; Pneumatic Separation System; robotic arm Embrace for debris removal
- Skyroot Aerospace: est. 2018, Hyderabad — founders Pawan Kumar Chandana, Naga Bharath Daka
- ISRO: 1969, Bengaluru, Chairman V. Narayanan; SDSC: est. 1971; IN-SPACe: 2020, Ahmedabad
- Mission Aagaman also carried a laboratory-grown, lotus-shaped diamond

### Quantum computing and National Quantum Mission
## 1. Why in news
- Google Willow processor (2024) — scaling 3×3 → 5×5 → 7×7 qubit lattices cut encoded error rate by factor of two each step
- IBM aims at fault-tolerant quantum computer by 2029
- Current era: NISQ (Noisy Intermediate-Scale Quantum)
## 2. Concepts
- Qubit: 0, 1 or both (superposition); entanglement; interference
- Error rates: quantum 1%–0.1%; classical one in a quintillion
- Quantum Error Correction: many physical qubits → one logical qubit
- QKD: photons; based on observer effect and no-cloning rule
| Qubit type | Note |
| Superconducting | Josephson junctions — Google Willow |
| Quantum dot | Semiconductor (silicon, GaAs, germanium) |
| Trapped ion | Charged atoms in EM fields |
| Photonic | Photons |
| NMR | Used in first quantum computer demonstration, 1998 |
- Nobel Physics 2025: John Clarke, Michel H. Devoret, John M. Martinis — macroscopic quantum tunnelling in electric circuit
## 3. National Quantum Mission
- Sanctioned: April 2023; outlay ₹6,000 crore+; target 2031
- Qubits: 20–50 in 3 years; 50–100 in 5 years; 50–1000 in 8 years
- Satellite quantum communication: 2,000 km; inter-city QKD 2,000 km
- T-Hubs: IISc Bengaluru (computing); IIT Madras + C-DOT (communication); IIT Bombay (sensing); IIT Delhi (materials)
- Model: Hub-Spoke-Spike; 14 Technical Groups, 17 States + 2 UTs
- ⚠ NQM covers communication, sensing, materials too — not only computing
- National Quantum Mission (2023-31): DST, ₹6,003.65 crore; targets 50-1,000 qubits
- DST quantum-safe report: post-quantum cryptography in critical infrastructure by 2029; other firms by 2033
- ⚠ NQM = DST; IndiaAI and ISM = MeitY

### Puga Valley — India's first geothermal wells
## 1. What happened
- ⚠ India's first and deepest geothermal wells operational at Puga Valley, Ladakh — about 1,000 m deep
- For 1 MW pilot — India's first demonstration-scale geothermal power project
- Executed by: ONGC Energy Centre; linked to Carbon-Neutral Ladakh
## 2. Background
- National Policy on Geothermal Energy, 2025: MNRE (task force 2024); supports Net Zero 2070
- India's potential: about 10.6 GW (GSI mapped hot springs)
- Sites: Ladakh — Puga, Chumathang, Demchok, Nubra; HP — Manikaran, Kasol, Tattapani; Uttarakhand — Tapoban; Sikkim — Yumesamdong; Gujarat — Dholera, Tuwa, Cambay Graben; Telangana — Manuguru; Andaman & Nicobar
- Plants: Dry steam; Flash; Binary (secondary low-boiling fluid — works at moderate temperatures)
- Enhanced Geothermal Systems: artificial permeability in hot dry rocks
- Geothermal gradient: rise of temperature with depth
- ONGC Energy Centre: est. 2005; ONGC: est. 1956
- Ladakh: UT since October 31, 2019; LG V. K. Saxena
- Inaugurated: July 17, 2026 by LG V. K. Saxena — two wells, 1,000 m deep; max 135°C recorded at 400 m depth
### China's helium export ban
## 1. What happened
- 10 July 2026: China imposed immediate temporary ban on helium exports
- ⚠ China is not a major producer — produces about 1.6% of world helium, imports 80%+
| Producer | Share |
| United States | about 43% |
## 2. Background
- Helium (He): atomic number 2; Group 18 noble gas; second-lightest; non-renewable
- Formed by: radioactive decay of uranium and thorium (alpha particles); extracted with natural gas (viable at 0.3% by volume); commercial purity 99.997%
- Boiling point: about -269°C (−268.9°C) — lowest of all; cannot solidify at normal pressure (needs ~25 atm at ~1 K)
- Uses: MRI magnets, semiconductor wafers, quantum computers, leak detection, optical fibres, rocket fuel-tank pressurisation (ISRO, NASA)
- Transport: vacuum-jacketed stainless steel vessels; boil-off risk
- Scuba tanks: air diluted with helium (avoids nitrogen narcosis)

### Space firsts
## Vikram-I (Skyroot)
- Vikram-I — India's first private orbital rocket; Skyroot Aerospace, Hyderabad

### India's space sovereignty: reforms and missions
## 1. Ecosystem
- Space start-ups: 1 (2014) to ~440 (2026)
- Space economy: USD 9 billion (Aug 2026); projected USD 40–45 billion in a decade
- Private investment: USD 100.5 million (FY22) to USD 618.5 million (Mar 2026)
- EO-PPP (IN-SPACe): ⚠ India's first privately owned Earth observation constellation; ~₹1,200 crore private commitment
- NSIL revenue: ₹321.77 crore (FY22) to ₹3,000+ crore (FY25)
- Foreign satellites launched: 35 before 2014 to 399 (Jan 2026); 300+ agreements with 61 countries
## 2. Reforms
- Sector opened to private players 2020; Indian Space Policy 2023
- FDI (Feb 2024): 74% automatic — satellite manufacturing/operations; 49% — launch vehicles, spaceports; 100% — components
- NGP 2024; ₹1,000 crore VC Fund; ₹500 crore Technology Adoption Fund; ₹75 crore Satellite Bus as a Service
## 3. Capabilities
- Launch vehicles: PSLV, GSLV, GSLV Mk III, SSLV — 12 missions July 2023–June 2026
- Sriharikota: 2 pads, third coming; Kulasekarapattinam (Tamil Nadu): second launch site (SSLV, private)
- Chandrayaan-3 (2023): first near south pole; 4th to soft-land
- Mars Orbiter: orbit 24 September 2014; XPoSat: January 2024
- SPADEX: January 2025 — India 4th nation to dock autonomously
- NavIC: coverage up to 1,500 km beyond India
## 4. Future missions
| Mission | Detail |
| Gaganyaan | Approved Jan 2019; expanded Sept 2024 to ₹20,193 crore, 8 missions; 3 astronauts, 400 km, 3 days; uncrewed Q4 2026, crewed 2027 |
| Bharatiya Antariksh Station | 5 modules; BAS-01 by 2028; full by 2035 |
| Chandrayaan-4 | Sample return, 2027 |
| Chandrayaan-5/LUPEX | With JAXA |
| Venus Orbiter Mission | March 2028 |
- Tech: 200-tonne semi-cryogenic engine; NGLV with LOX-methane
## 5. Gaganyaan thermal shield and systems
- Ablative materials: carbon phenolic, silica phenolic — single use
- HLVM3: ~53 m, ~640 t, 3 stages, ~10 t to LEO
- Orbital Module: pressurised Crew Module + unpressurised Service Module (separates before re-entry)
- Parachutes: 10 of 4 types (apex cover, drogue, pilot, main); sea splashdown
- Milestones: SRE-1 (2007); LVM3-X/CARE (2014); Pad Abort Test (2018); TV-D1; Integrated Air Drop Tests
- ⚠ Re-entry heat comes mainly from compression of air, not friction
## 6. Private space updates (CA-360)
- EO constellation: IN-SPACe signed for India's first fully commercial EO constellation (PPP) — Allied Orbits consortium led by PixxelSpace, with Dhruva Space, PierSight, SatSure; 12 satellites (panchromatic, multispectral, hyperspectral, SAR); ₹1,200 crore+; by 2029 (Dr Jitendra Singh in Lok Sabha)
- PowerBank-50: ⚠ India's first satellite battery with "Flight Heritage" — TakeMe2Space (Hyderabad); flew on Skyroot's Vikram-1 "Mission Aagaman", 18 July 2026; 50+ Wh, 380 g
- Powered Cosmoserve Space's payload at 450 km; designed for LEO (160–2,000 km) life of up to 5 years
- IN-SPACe: est. 2020, HQ Ahmedabad

### Vikram-1 — India's first private orbital launch
## 1. What happened
- Stacking (early July): first stage of Vikram-I stacked at First Launch Pad, SDSC Sriharikota — ⚠ first privately built orbital-class rocket stacked there
- Launch: 18 July 2026, 12:05:30 PM, SDSC Sriharikota — ⚠ India's first private orbital rocket launch from Indian soil
- Developer: Skyroot Aerospace, Hyderabad
- Mission name: Mission Aagaman
- Orbit: about 450 km LEO, payload injected about 15 minutes after lift-off
- ⚠ India became third country after USA and China with private orbital launch capability
## 2. Vehicle
| Feature | Detail |
| Stages | 4 — 3 solid + liquid upper stage (orbital adjustment module) |
| Upper-stage engine | RAMAN-I — 3D-printed liquid engine (tested at LPSC) |
| Payload | up to 350 kg to LEO |
## 3. Payloads
| Payload | Purpose |
| SCOPE | Skyroot's own satellite — flight data |
| Grahaa / SOLARAS S3 | Earth observation / tech |
| DCUBED | German tech demonstration |
| Cosmoserve Embrace | Robotic arm — debris capture |
| Cosmic Bloom | Art payload |
| 18-karat gold micro-rocket | Tribute to Indian scientists |
## 4. Background
- Vikram-S (2022): ⚠ sub-orbital — first private Indian rocket to reach space; Vikram-1 is orbital
- Agnikul Cosmos (2024): launched from India's first private launch pad Dhanush
- SSLV ToT to HAL: February 2026
- Indian Space Policy 2023; sector opened 2020
- IN-SPACe (June 2026): 4,500+ organisations registered, 133 authorisations, 106 MoUs
- NSIL (July 2026): 141 satellites launched (138 customer, 3 Indian)
- Funds: IN-SPACe Seed Fund; ₹1,000 crore VC Fund; ₹500 crore Technology Adoption Fund (up to 60% of project cost)
- LEO: 160–2,000 km; orbit period 90–120 min
- Second spaceport: Kulasekarapattinam, Tamil Nadu; Sriharikota near Pulicat Lake
## 5. More details (CA360)
- Three solid stages + liquid Orbital Adjustment Module (OAM); diameter about 1.7 m (⚠ CA360 gives height about 23 m; daily source 22 m)
- Firsts: all-carbon composite structure; first 3D-printed liquid engine in an Indian rocket; Pneumatic Separation System; robotic arm Embrace for debris removal
- Skyroot Aerospace: est. 2018, Hyderabad — founders Pawan Kumar Chandana, Naga Bharath Daka
- ISRO: 1969, Bengaluru, Chairman V. Narayanan; SDSC: est. 1971; IN-SPACe: 2020, Ahmedabad
- Mission Aagaman also carried a laboratory-grown, lotus-shaped diamond

### Puga Valley — India's first geothermal wells
## 1. What happened
- ⚠ India's first and deepest geothermal wells operational at Puga Valley, Ladakh — about 1,000 m deep
- For 1 MW pilot — India's first demonstration-scale geothermal power project
- Executed by: ONGC Energy Centre; linked to Carbon-Neutral Ladakh
## 2. Background
- National Policy on Geothermal Energy, 2025: MNRE (task force 2024); supports Net Zero 2070
- India's potential: about 10.6 GW (GSI mapped hot springs)
- Sites: Ladakh — Puga, Chumathang, Demchok, Nubra; HP — Manikaran, Kasol, Tattapani; Uttarakhand — Tapoban; Sikkim — Yumesamdong; Gujarat — Dholera, Tuwa, Cambay Graben; Telangana — Manuguru; Andaman & Nicobar
- Plants: Dry steam; Flash; Binary (secondary low-boiling fluid — works at moderate temperatures)
- Enhanced Geothermal Systems: artificial permeability in hot dry rocks
- Geothermal gradient: rise of temperature with depth
- ONGC Energy Centre: est. 2005; ONGC: est. 1956
- Ladakh: UT since October 31, 2019; LG V. K. Saxena
- Inaugurated: July 17, 2026 by LG V. K. Saxena — two wells, 1,000 m deep; max 135°C recorded at 400 m depth
### China's helium export ban
## 1. What happened
- 10 July 2026: China imposed immediate temporary ban on helium exports
- ⚠ China is not a major producer — produces about 1.6% of world helium, imports 80%+
| Producer | Share |
| United States | about 43% |
## 2. Background
- Helium (He): atomic number 2; Group 18 noble gas; second-lightest; non-renewable
- Formed by: radioactive decay of uranium and thorium (alpha particles); extracted with natural gas (viable at 0.3% by volume); commercial purity 99.997%
- Boiling point: about -269°C (−268.9°C) — lowest of all; cannot solidify at normal pressure (needs ~25 atm at ~1 K)
- Uses: MRI magnets, semiconductor wafers, quantum computers, leak detection, optical fibres, rocket fuel-tank pressurisation (ISRO, NASA)
- Transport: vacuum-jacketed stainless steel vessels; boil-off risk
- Scuba tanks: air diluted with helium (avoids nitrogen narcosis)

### Operation Sindoor — lessons and nuclear doctrine
## 1. Key points
- India's calibrated response after Pahalgam terror attack — "aggression with restraint"; multi-domain (cyber, electronic warfare, intelligence fusion, precision strikes)
- Grey zone warfare: hostile acts below threshold of conventional war
## 2. India's Nuclear Doctrine
- Adopted: January 2003 (after Pokhran-II)
- Credible Minimum Deterrence; No First Use (NFU); massive retaliation
- Authorised by: civilian Nuclear Command Authority (NCA)
- No use against non-nuclear weapon states

## 1. New system

- Monsoon normally reaches Mumbai ~10 June, Delhi ~29 June

- Orbital dependency: reliance on foreign satellite infrastructure

### Gaganyaan — safe return mechanism
## 1. Mission basics
- India's first human spaceflight — 3 astronauts, LEO ~400 km, up to 3 days
- Launcher: human-rated LVM3 (GSLV Mk III) — solid, liquid, cryogenic stages (⚠ not PSLV)
- Orbital Module = Crew Module (pressurised, habitable, thermal protection) + Service Module (unpressurised; separates before re-entry)
- Crew Escape System: validated by TV-D1; uses solid rocket motors; works during launch/ascent (⚠ not re-entry)
- Vyommitra: female humanoid robot for uncrewed missions
- Vyommitra: semi-humanoid; flies on uncrewed G1/G2
- India would join USA, Russia, China with independent human spaceflight capability
## 2. Return sequence
| Step | Detail |
| Re-entry / aerobraking | Drag reduces speed from ~7.8 km/s |
| Splashdown | Bay of Bengal; sea absorbs 7–9 m/s impact (vs 1–2 m/s on land) |
- Tests: IADT (Integrated Air Drop Test), Pad Abort Test, Test Vehicle flights
- Gaganyaan G1: uncrewed G1 flight "launching shortly" (Aug 2026); crew-module tests done; ESA will give ground-tracking support
## 3. Aditya-L1 (related)
- India's first solar observatory at L1 (~1.5 million km); ISRO's second observatory-class mission after AstroSat (2015); ~125 days to L1 (vs ~300 days for MOM)
### Kalpakkam PFBR attains criticality — stage II of nuclear programme
## 1. News
- Prototype Fast Breeder Reactor (PFBR), Kalpakkam (Tamil Nadu) attained criticality — PM Narendra Modi called it historic
- 500 MW, sodium-cooled; built by BHAVINI (Bharatiya Nabhikiya Vidyut Nigam Ltd, under DAE); 200+ Indian industries
## 2. Concepts
- Criticality: self-sustaining chain reaction — k = 1 (subcritical k<1, supercritical k>1)
- FBR: produces more fissile fuel than it consumes; fast neutrons, no moderator; MOX fuel (Pu + U); U-238 blanket → Pu-239; liquid sodium coolant (low dissolved oxygen for corrosion resistance)
- Chain reaction idea — Leo Szilard (1933)
## 3. Three-stage programme (Homi J. Bhabha)
| Stage | Reactor | Fuel |
| I | PHWR | Natural uranium → Pu-239 by-product |
| II | FBR | Pu-based MOX |
| III | Thorium reactors | Th-232 → U-233 |
- Plants: Tarapur, Rajasthan, Kalpakkam, Narora, Kakrapar, Kaiga, Kudankulam; Kalpakkam has MAPS, IGCAR
### SHANTI Act 2025 and 100 GW nuclear target by 2047
## 1. Targets
- Nuclear capacity: ~8,180 MW → 100 GW by 2047 (over twelvefold); investment > $200 billion
- Installed electricity capacity (June 2025): ~476 GW, ~50% non-fossil
## 2. SHANTI Act, 2025 — key provisions
- Full form: Sustainable Harnessing and Advancement of Nuclear Energy for Transforming India
- Replaces Atomic Energy Act, 1962 and Civil Liability for Nuclear Damage Act, 2010
- Private companies, JVs, foreign entities can build, own, operate, decommission plants — ends NPCIL monopoly
- Operator solely liable; ⚠ supplier liability removed; operator liability graded by capacity; govt liability capped
- Statutory status to Atomic Energy Regulatory Board (AERB)
## 3. SMRs and strategy
- ⚠ Another source: nuclear capacity 8.8 GW → 100 GW by 2047; nuclear share ~3%; NPCIL 1987, DAE 1954, AERB 1983 (all Mumbai)

### Anthropic's Mythos AI model and cybersecurity
## 1. News
- Mythos — AI model by Anthropic — can autonomously identify and exploit software vulnerabilities
- Not publicly released; shared selectively with critical-infrastructure organisations under Project Glasswing
## 2. Key terms
- Zero-day vulnerability: flaw unknown to developers, no patch (⚠ not already patched)
- LLMs: transformer architecture; temperature controls randomness; tokenization — words/subwords/characters (does not decide architecture); loss function measures prediction error
- Adversarial attacks, data poisoning; XAI (Explainable AI); EDR systems; deepfakes
### Quantum entanglement in heavier atoms
## 1. Key facts
- Quantum entanglement: shared quantum state — measuring one particle's property (momentum, spin) instantly fixes its partner's, regardless of distance
- Earlier shown mainly in photons and electrons; recently demonstrated in heavier atoms like Helium
- ⚠ Nonlocality is a quantum feature — classical physics is based on locality
### Space governance and commercialisation of outer space
## 1. Legal framework
| Instrument | Key point |
| Outer Space Treaty, 1967 | Foundation of space law; states responsible for national space activities incl. private entities; space = global commons |
| Liability Convention, 1972 | Compensation for damage by space objects — absolute liability on Earth/aircraft; fault-based in outer space |
| IADC, UN COPUOS guidelines | Debris mitigation — ⚠ non-binding, voluntary |
## 2. Exam angle
- Kessler Syndrome: chain reaction of debris collisions making orbits unusable
- Only objects >10 cm in LEO consistently tracked; 25-year de-orbit rule — weak compliance monitoring
### Keytruda (Pembrolizumab) and counterfeit cancer drugs
## 1. News
- Counterfeit Keytruda market reported in India — high cost (> ₹3 lakh/month), weak procurement oversight
- Govt removed basic customs duty; company Patient Access Programme (buy 5 doses → 30 free)
## 2. Science
- Developed by Merck & Co. (MSD); US FDA approval 2014
- Monoclonal antibody, immune checkpoint inhibitor — blocks PD-1 receptor on T-cells
- PD-1–PD-L1 binding switches off T-cells; blocking reactivates immunity — ⚠ does not directly kill cancer cells
- Treats: lung, cervical, renal cell carcinoma, triple-negative breast cancer
- Other immunotherapies: CAR-T cell therapy, mRNA cancer vaccines

### NavIC satellite IRNSS-1F loses atomic clock
## 1. News
- ISRO: atomic clock on IRNSS-1F stopped on 13 March 2026 — no positioning data; continues broadcast messaging
- IRNSS-1F launched March 2016; completed 10-year design life
- After NVS-01 (2023), five satellites gave positioning: IRNSS-1B, 1C, 1F, 1I, NVS-01
## 2. NavIC basics
- Official name: IRNSS; planned 7-satellite constellation; coverage India + 1,500 km; accuracy ~10 m
- ⚠ Regional systems use geosynchronous orbits (~35,000 km); global systems MEO (~20,000 km)
- New-generation (NVS): indigenous atomic clock; 12-year life; L1, L5, S bands — L1 enables smartphones/wearables
## 3. Setbacks
- NVS-02: launched January 2025 by GSLV-F15 — ⚠ ISRO's 100th launch; failed to reach final orbit — signal did not reach pyro valve of oxidiser line (connector disengagement)
- IRNSS-1H (2017) failed — heat shield did not open; IRNSS-1A (2013) almost defunct
## 4. Navigation systems
| System | Country |
| GPS | USA |
| GLONASS | Russia |
| Galileo | EU |
| BeiDou | China |
| NavIC (regional) | India |
| QZSS (regional) | Japan |
- GAGAN: India's SBAS — ISRO + Airports Authority of India; ⚠ not limited to aviation
## 5. More facts
- IRNSS-1F launched 10 March 2016; ⚠ all three atomic clocks non-functional; NavIC needs at least four operational satellites; services began 2018

## 1. Mission
- Artemis II: four astronauts around the Moon and back — ⚠ no landing; first crewed lunar flyby in 50+ years; Orion spacecraft

| Artemis II (2026) | Crewed flyby |

| Artemis IV, V | 2028–29, 2030 |
- NASA aims for a Moon landing once every six months; permanent lunar base

- Apollo: first landing 1969; 12 astronauts walked on the Moon in six missions (1969–72); ISRO founded within a month of Apollo 11
- ISS: ~400 km up, manned ~25 years; to retire in 3–4 years; Moon ~400,000 km

### Missile interceptors in the US–Iran war
## 1. News
- Expanded IAMD network: US + Israel + UAE against Iran — different from the Twelve-Day War (June 2025), when Iran fired 500+ ballistic missiles and twice as many suicide drones
- UAE: deployed South Korean Cheongung II over the Gulf
- US: IFPC (Indirect Fire Protection Capability) using AIM-9X Sidewinder to reduce reliance on Patriot; SM-6 in dual role (terminal intercept + fast-attack craft)
- Israel: Iron Beam (laser) as primary defence against drone swarms to conserve Arrow 3 and Stunner
## 2. Layered defence (2025 sequence)
| Layer | System |
| Exo-atmospheric (first line) | Israel's Arrow 3 + US destroyers with SM-3 |
| Endo-atmospheric | THAAD + Arrow 2 |
| Mid layer | David's Sling with Stunner |
| Last line | Patriot |
| Against drones | Iron Dome (Tamir) + Iron Beam |
## 3. Key numbers
- Patriot PAC-3 MSE: ~$4 million per interceptor — cost asymmetry vs cheap drones (saturation attacks)
- Patriot once hit 100% of six hypersonic Kinzhals over Kyiv; fell to ~10% against Russian decoys/manoeuvres
- US quadrupled production orders for THAAD and PAC-3 MSE; THAAD replenishment ≥ 1.5 years
## 4. Cheongung II features
- Hit-to-kill; 360° firing via Vertical Launch System + rotating multi-function radar
- ⚠ Older Patriot radars scanned only a 120° cone; Patriot radar is stationary, not rotating
- Nose radar in final seconds to counter low-flying "skimmers"
## 5. Iran's air defence
| System | Role |
| Bavar-373 (Sayyad-4B) | Most advanced, long-range — 300+ km; aircraft and ballistic missiles |
| Arman BMD | Short/medium-range ballistic missiles, 360° radar |
| Sevom-e-Khordad | Mobile, cruise missiles/jets; Sayyad-3 guards Natanz, Isfahan |
| Tor-M1 (Russia-made) | Precision-guided bombs |
| Majid, Azarakhsh | Low-flying drones, cruise missiles |
- ⚠ THAAD = Terminal High Altitude Area Defence — an American anti-missile system (UPSC PYQ)

### Anthropic study on AI and jobs — Observed Exposure
## 1. News
- Anthropic labour-market study on LLMs introduced "Observed Exposure" — actual workplace use of AI, not just theoretical capability
- Data: US occupational database (~800 occupations), academic research, Claude AI usage data
## 2. Findings
- Most exposed: computer programmers, financial analysts, customer service, data analysts, legal, office administration
- Least exposed: construction, agriculture, protective services, personal care
- LLMs can theoretically do ~94% of tasks in computer/maths jobs; actual use ~33%
- Entry of 22–25-year-olds into exposed jobs down ~14% since 2022 — less entry-level hiring, not layoffs
- Exposed workers: 54.4% female, highly educated, average age ~42.9
- India: AI could reduce IT services revenue by 9–12% over four years; Nifty IT fell
### Gut microbiome and food systems
## 1. Gut microbiome facts
- Trillions of microbes (~equal to human cells); weight 1–2 kg (like the brain)
- 2–20 million microbial genes vs ~20,000 human genes; ~2,000 bacterial species, mostly in the colon
- ~90% beneficial — Bacteroidetes, Firmicutes; unique like a fingerprint
- Dysbiosis → "leaky gut", inflammation; SCFAs (short-chain fatty acids) reduce inflammation; synthesises vitamin K and some B vitamins; gut–brain axis
## 2. Disruptors
- Sugar favours E. coli, Candida; reduces Bifidobacteria, Lactobacilli
- Estrobolome — gut bacteria regulating estrogen metabolism; disrupted by pesticides
- Antibiotics in livestock → AMR; ⚠ bacteria, viruses and fungi can all develop AMR
- Fat digested by lipases; proteins by trypsin/chymotrypsin; starch by amylase

### Missile & weapon tests
## Indian systems and tests
- Operation Sindoor — indigenous Akashteer, Akash missile system and BrahMos
- TARA — India's first indigenous Guided Glide Weapon (turns unguided warheads into precision-guided munitions)
- Suryaastra guided long-range rocket — by Nibe Limited; tested from Chandipur; 150 km and 300 km variants; accuracy measured by Circular Error Probable
- HSTDV — DRDO's hypersonic technology demonstrator programme
- PGM-V3 — UAV-launched precision guided missile; tested at DRDO's Kurnool test facility; air-to-surface and air-to-air variants
- Vayu Baan — IAF's first helicopter-launched drone system; strikes beyond 50 km
- Two new military vehicles (one tracked, one wheeled) — DRDO's VRDE, Ahilyanagar
## Other facts
- Tarapur Units 1 and 2 (TAPS) — boiling water reactors (BWRs), not PHWRs; commissioned 1969, India's first commercial reactors
- Gaganyaan — India would join USA, Russia and China in independent human spaceflight capability
- Digital India — launched by MeitY, July 2015; three vision areas and nine pillars
- UAE deployed South Korean Cheongung II over the Gulf
### Indian space sector
## Gaganyaan
- Profile: three astronauts to LEO at about 400 km for up to three days
- Launcher: human-rated LVM3 (not PSLV)
- Crew Escape System — works during launch and ascent (not re-entry)

### Gaganyaan — safe return mechanism
## 1. Mission basics
- India's first human spaceflight — 3 astronauts, LEO ~400 km, up to 3 days
- Launcher: human-rated LVM3 (GSLV Mk III) — solid, liquid, cryogenic stages (⚠ not PSLV)
- Orbital Module = Crew Module (pressurised, habitable, thermal protection) + Service Module (unpressurised; separates before re-entry)
- Crew Escape System: validated by TV-D1; uses solid rocket motors; works during launch/ascent (⚠ not re-entry)
- Vyommitra: female humanoid robot for uncrewed missions
- Vyommitra: semi-humanoid; flies on uncrewed G1/G2
- India would join USA, Russia, China with independent human spaceflight capability
## 2. Return sequence
| Step | Detail |
| Re-entry / aerobraking | Drag reduces speed from ~7.8 km/s |
| Splashdown | Bay of Bengal; sea absorbs 7–9 m/s impact (vs 1–2 m/s on land) |
- Tests: IADT (Integrated Air Drop Test), Pad Abort Test, Test Vehicle flights
- Gaganyaan G1: uncrewed G1 flight "launching shortly" (Aug 2026); crew-module tests done; ESA will give ground-tracking support
## 3. Aditya-L1 (related)
- India's first solar observatory at L1 (~1.5 million km); ISRO's second observatory-class mission after AstroSat (2015); ~125 days to L1 (vs ~300 days for MOM)
### Kalpakkam PFBR attains criticality — stage II of nuclear programme
## 1. News
- Prototype Fast Breeder Reactor (PFBR), Kalpakkam (Tamil Nadu) attained criticality — PM Narendra Modi called it historic
- 500 MW, sodium-cooled; built by BHAVINI (Bharatiya Nabhikiya Vidyut Nigam Ltd, under DAE); 200+ Indian industries
## 2. Concepts
- Criticality: self-sustaining chain reaction — k = 1 (subcritical k<1, supercritical k>1)
- FBR: produces more fissile fuel than it consumes; fast neutrons, no moderator; MOX fuel (Pu + U); U-238 blanket → Pu-239; liquid sodium coolant (low dissolved oxygen for corrosion resistance)
- Chain reaction idea — Leo Szilard (1933)
## 3. Three-stage programme (Homi J. Bhabha)
| Stage | Reactor | Fuel |
| I | PHWR | Natural uranium → Pu-239 by-product |
| II | FBR | Pu-based MOX |
| III | Thorium reactors | Th-232 → U-233 |
- Plants: Tarapur, Rajasthan, Kalpakkam, Narora, Kakrapar, Kaiga, Kudankulam; Kalpakkam has MAPS, IGCAR
### SHANTI Act 2025 and 100 GW nuclear target by 2047
## 1. Targets
- Nuclear capacity: ~8,180 MW → 100 GW by 2047 (over twelvefold); investment > $200 billion
- Installed electricity capacity (June 2025): ~476 GW, ~50% non-fossil
## 2. SHANTI Act, 2025 — key provisions
- Full form: Sustainable Harnessing and Advancement of Nuclear Energy for Transforming India
- Replaces Atomic Energy Act, 1962 and Civil Liability for Nuclear Damage Act, 2010
- Private companies, JVs, foreign entities can build, own, operate, decommission plants — ends NPCIL monopoly
- Operator solely liable; ⚠ supplier liability removed; operator liability graded by capacity; govt liability capped
- Statutory status to Atomic Energy Regulatory Board (AERB)
## 3. SMRs and strategy
- ⚠ Another source: nuclear capacity 8.8 GW → 100 GW by 2047; nuclear share ~3%; NPCIL 1987, DAE 1954, AERB 1983 (all Mumbai)

### Space governance and commercialisation of outer space
## 1. Legal framework
| Instrument | Key point |
| Outer Space Treaty, 1967 | Foundation of space law; states responsible for national space activities incl. private entities; space = global commons |
| Liability Convention, 1972 | Compensation for damage by space objects — absolute liability on Earth/aircraft; fault-based in outer space |
| IADC, UN COPUOS guidelines | Debris mitigation — ⚠ non-binding, voluntary |
## 2. Exam angle
- Kessler Syndrome: chain reaction of debris collisions making orbits unusable
- Only objects >10 cm in LEO consistently tracked; 25-year de-orbit rule — weak compliance monitoring
### Keytruda (Pembrolizumab) and counterfeit cancer drugs
## 1. News
- Counterfeit Keytruda market reported in India — high cost (> ₹3 lakh/month), weak procurement oversight
- Govt removed basic customs duty; company Patient Access Programme (buy 5 doses → 30 free)
## 2. Science
- Developed by Merck & Co. (MSD); US FDA approval 2014
- Monoclonal antibody, immune checkpoint inhibitor — blocks PD-1 receptor on T-cells
- PD-1–PD-L1 binding switches off T-cells; blocking reactivates immunity — ⚠ does not directly kill cancer cells
- Treats: lung, cervical, renal cell carcinoma, triple-negative breast cancer
- Other immunotherapies: CAR-T cell therapy, mRNA cancer vaccines

### NavIC satellite IRNSS-1F loses atomic clock
## 1. News
- ISRO: atomic clock on IRNSS-1F stopped on 13 March 2026 — no positioning data; continues broadcast messaging
- IRNSS-1F launched March 2016; completed 10-year design life
- After NVS-01 (2023), five satellites gave positioning: IRNSS-1B, 1C, 1F, 1I, NVS-01
## 2. NavIC basics
- Official name: IRNSS; planned 7-satellite constellation; coverage India + 1,500 km; accuracy ~10 m
- ⚠ Regional systems use geosynchronous orbits (~35,000 km); global systems MEO (~20,000 km)
- New-generation (NVS): indigenous atomic clock; 12-year life; L1, L5, S bands — L1 enables smartphones/wearables
## 3. Setbacks
- NVS-02: launched January 2025 by GSLV-F15 — ⚠ ISRO's 100th launch; failed to reach final orbit — signal did not reach pyro valve of oxidiser line (connector disengagement)
- IRNSS-1H (2017) failed — heat shield did not open; IRNSS-1A (2013) almost defunct
## 4. Navigation systems
| System | Country |
| GPS | USA |
| GLONASS | Russia |
| Galileo | EU |
| BeiDou | China |
| NavIC (regional) | India |
| QZSS (regional) | Japan |
- GAGAN: India's SBAS — ISRO + Airports Authority of India; ⚠ not limited to aviation
## 5. More facts
- IRNSS-1F launched 10 March 2016; ⚠ all three atomic clocks non-functional; NavIC needs at least four operational satellites; services began 2018

### Missile interceptors in the US–Iran war
## 1. News
- Expanded IAMD network: US + Israel + UAE against Iran — different from the Twelve-Day War (June 2025), when Iran fired 500+ ballistic missiles and twice as many suicide drones
- UAE: deployed South Korean Cheongung II over the Gulf
- US: IFPC (Indirect Fire Protection Capability) using AIM-9X Sidewinder to reduce reliance on Patriot; SM-6 in dual role (terminal intercept + fast-attack craft)
- Israel: Iron Beam (laser) as primary defence against drone swarms to conserve Arrow 3 and Stunner
## 2. Layered defence (2025 sequence)
| Layer | System |
| Exo-atmospheric (first line) | Israel's Arrow 3 + US destroyers with SM-3 |
| Endo-atmospheric | THAAD + Arrow 2 |
| Mid layer | David's Sling with Stunner |
| Last line | Patriot |
| Against drones | Iron Dome (Tamir) + Iron Beam |
## 3. Key numbers
- Patriot PAC-3 MSE: ~$4 million per interceptor — cost asymmetry vs cheap drones (saturation attacks)
- Patriot once hit 100% of six hypersonic Kinzhals over Kyiv; fell to ~10% against Russian decoys/manoeuvres
- US quadrupled production orders for THAAD and PAC-3 MSE; THAAD replenishment ≥ 1.5 years
## 4. Cheongung II features
- Hit-to-kill; 360° firing via Vertical Launch System + rotating multi-function radar
- ⚠ Older Patriot radars scanned only a 120° cone; Patriot radar is stationary, not rotating
- Nose radar in final seconds to counter low-flying "skimmers"
## 5. Iran's air defence
| System | Role |
| Bavar-373 (Sayyad-4B) | Most advanced, long-range — 300+ km; aircraft and ballistic missiles |
| Arman BMD | Short/medium-range ballistic missiles, 360° radar |
| Sevom-e-Khordad | Mobile, cruise missiles/jets; Sayyad-3 guards Natanz, Isfahan |
| Tor-M1 (Russia-made) | Precision-guided bombs |
| Majid, Azarakhsh | Low-flying drones, cruise missiles |
- ⚠ THAAD = Terminal High Altitude Area Defence — an American anti-missile system (UPSC PYQ)

### Gut microbiome and food systems
## 1. Gut microbiome facts
- Trillions of microbes (~equal to human cells); weight 1–2 kg (like the brain)
- 2–20 million microbial genes vs ~20,000 human genes; ~2,000 bacterial species, mostly in the colon
- ~90% beneficial — Bacteroidetes, Firmicutes; unique like a fingerprint
- Dysbiosis → "leaky gut", inflammation; SCFAs (short-chain fatty acids) reduce inflammation; synthesises vitamin K and some B vitamins; gut–brain axis
## 2. Disruptors
- Sugar favours E. coli, Candida; reduces Bifidobacteria, Lactobacilli
- Estrobolome — gut bacteria regulating estrogen metabolism; disrupted by pesticides
- Antibiotics in livestock → AMR; ⚠ bacteria, viruses and fungi can all develop AMR
- Fat digested by lipases; proteins by trypsin/chymotrypsin; starch by amylase

### Sodium-ion batteries — case for India
## 1. Lithium-ion status
- Li-ion price: ~$1,100/kWh (early 2010s) → ~$108/kWh (2025)
- PLI for Advanced Chemistry Cells (2021): ~40 GWh allocated, only ~1 GWh commissioned
- Cathode: lithium metal oxides — LiCoO₂, LiMn₂O₄, LiFePO₄; ⚠ anode = graphite (graphite is not a cathode material)
## 2. Sodium-ion advantages
- Energy density approaching LFP; layered-oxide chemistries outperform polyanionic and Prussian blue analogues
- Safety: lower thermal-runaway temperature; ⚠ not "Dangerous Goods" for transport; can be stored/shipped at zero volts (Li-ion at ≤30% charge)
- Aluminium current collectors on both electrodes (Li-ion uses copper); sodium from soda ash
- Global capacity: ~70 GWh (2025) → ~400 GWh by 2030; expected to undercut Li-ion costs by 2035
### PERT — single genome-editing strategy for many disorders
## 1. Study
- Journal: Nature; by Broad Institute, Harvard, University of Minnesota
- PERT: Prime-Editing-mediated Readthrough of Premature Termination codons — edits a tRNA gene into a suppressor tRNA
- Target: nonsense mutations (premature stop codon, e.g. TAG) — ~25% of disease-causing genetic changes
## 2. Key numbers
- Human cells have 418 tRNA genes; 4 promising — leucine, arginine, tyrosine, serine
- 17,000+ configurations tested; enzyme PE6c + PE3 strategy
- Efficiency: 60–80% vs HDR 10–20%
- Mouse models: Batten, Tay-Sachs, Niemann-Pick C1; Hurler syndrome — activity restored to 1.7% of normal

### Lenacapavir — Zimbabwe rollout and HIV capsid science
## 1. Drug
- ⚠ First twice-yearly injectable PrEP for HIV — developed by Gilead Sciences
- US FDA approved on 18 June 2025 — world's first capsid-based HIV inhibitor; 100% effective in trials
- ⚠ Not a vaccine; only for HIV-negative persons; does not replace ART
## 2. Zimbabwe
- Rollout 19 February 2026 at Epworth (~20 km south of Harare); 46,000 doses, 24 sites
- Funded by US government and Global Fund; Zimbabwe among 9 countries chosen by WHO for early access
- Zimbabwe PrEP toolkit: oral PrEP (2016), dapivirine vaginal ring (2021), injectable cabotegravir (2024), lenacapavir (2026)
## 3. Science
- Resistant HIV replicates at only 20–30% of normal — capsid is a high-constraint target; capsid shape paper 1999 (Science)
- AZT (zidovudine), 1987 — first drug, targeted reverse transcriptase
- Global: ~41 million living with HIV; ~27 million (67%) in Sub-Saharan Africa; 44 million+ deaths since 1980s
- ⚠ Tattooing can transmit Hepatitis B and HIV — not Chikungunya
## 4. CA360 details
- Brand name "Yeztugo"; ⚠ CA360 cites ~99.9% effectiveness (daily: 100% in trials); Gilead est. 1987, Foster City

## 1. News

- Duration: 108 seconds; all parameters matched — qualified for flight missions
## 2. SSLV and SS3
- SSLV: three-stage, all-solid vehicle; up to about 500 kg to LEO; launch-on-demand
- SS3: gives up to 4 km/s velocity
- Carbon-epoxy motor case → payload gain about 90 kg
- Motor case: made at VSSC Composites Entity; casting/testing at SDSC

### DHRUV64 — India's first indigenous 1.0 GHz 64-bit dual-core microprocessor
## 1. News
- DHRUV64: India's first fully indigenous 1.0 GHz, 64-bit dual-core microprocessor
- Developer: C-DAC, under the Microprocessor Development Programme (MDP)
- Architecture: open-source RISC-V — no licence costs
- ⚠ Third chip fabricated under Digital India RISC-V (DIR-V) Programme
- Uses: 5G infrastructure, automotive, consumer electronics, industrial automation, IoT
- India: consumes about 20% of world's microprocessors; has 20% of world's chip design engineers
- Next: Dhanush and Dhanush+ (DHANUSH64, DHANUSH64+ SoC variants)
## 2. Indigenous processors
| Processor | Year | Developer | Use |

| VIKRAM | 2025 | ISRO–SCL | Space navigation, guidance |
| THEJAS32 | — | C-DAC | Fabricated at Silterra, Malaysia |
| THEJAS64 | 2025 | C-DAC | Industrial automation; made at SCL Mohali |
- ⚠ VIKRAM is ISRO–SCL, not C-DAC
- SHAKTI aerospace chip (Feb 2025): built on IIT Madras's SHAKTI processor with ISRO
- ⚠ Trap: not IIT Delhi (PYQ)
- VEGA (2023): C-DAC's first indigenous processor series (RISC-V); DIR-V: Digital India RISC-V programme of MeitY

- New laws 2025: Indian Ports Act, 2025 (replaces 1908 Act), Merchant Shipping Act, 2025, Coastal Shipping Act, 2025; Maritime India Vision 2030
## 3. Port facts
- Kamarajar Port (Ennore): first major port registered as a company
- Mundra: largest private port (APSEZ, Gulf of Kutch)
- ⚠ Largest container port: JNPT (Nhava Sheva), not Visakhapatnam
### Rare-earth elements — why everyone wants them
## 1. Basics
- REEs: 17 elements — 15 lanthanides (lanthanum to lutetium) + scandium and yttrium (Group 3)
- ⚠ Not geologically rare — cerium as abundant as copper; rarity is economic/technological (hard separation)
- ⚠ Lithium, cobalt, gallium, germanium are critical minerals, not REEs
- Properties: from localised 4f electrons — strong magnetism, sharp phosphor emission
## 2. Uses
| Use | Elements |
| Permanent magnets (NdFeB) | Neodymium — EV motors, wind turbines |
| Phosphors (LEDs, displays) | Europium, terbium, yttrium |
| Lasers, fibre optics | Neodymium, erbium |
| Refining catalysts | Lanthanum, cerium |
- Ores: bastnäsite, monazite, ion-adsorption clays; may contain thorium/uranium
## 3. Reserves and processing (USGS)
| Country | Reserves (MT REO) |
| China | 44 |
| Brazil | 21 |
| India | 6.9 |
- World reserves: over 90 million tonnes
- China: about 91% of separation/refining; 94% of sintered permanent magnets
- Japan: plans to extract REE-rich mud from 6 km underwater near Minamitori Island in early 2026
### Nuclear power in space — US lunar fission reactor
## 1. News
- US plan: Lunar Fission Surface Power Project — small fission reactor on the Moon by early 2030s, for NASA's Artemis Base Camp
- ⚠ Would be the first permanent nuclear power source beyond Earth orbit
## 2. Space nuclear technologies
| Technology | Key point |
| Compact fission reactor | Shipping-container size; 10–100 kW; NASA KRUSTY demo up to 10 kW |
| Nuclear Thermal Propulsion (NTP) | Reactor heats propellant (liquid hydrogen); high thrust; US DRACO test by 2026 |
| Nuclear Electric Propulsion (NEP) | Reactor electricity powers ion/plasma thrusters; low continuous thrust |
## 3. Legal framework
- Outer Space Treaty 1967, Article IV: bans nuclear weapons/WMDs in orbit and on celestial bodies — ⚠ does not ban peaceful nuclear power
- UN Principles on Nuclear Power Sources in Outer Space, 1992 (UNGA Res. 47/68): non-binding; ⚠ ignore NTP/NEP propulsion
- 1972 Liability Convention: absolute liability for damage on Earth/aircraft; fault-based in outer space
- COPUOS: UN Committee on the Peaceful Uses of Outer Space
- NPT Nuclear Weapon States: USA, Russia, UK, France, China — India, Pakistan, Israel not recognised
- ⚠ NSG membership does not mean automatic NPT membership
### Neurotechnology and UNESCO's ethics framework
## 1. News
- Context: rapid growth of Brain–Computer Interfaces (BCIs); UNESCO 2025 — world's first comprehensive ethical framework for neurotechnology
- BCI types: non-invasive (EEG headsets) and invasive (implanted electrodes)
- India: stroke is the largest contributor to neurological disability
## 2. India's ecosystem
- IIT Kanpur: BCI-based robotic hand for stroke patients
- National Brain Research Centre (Manesar); IISc Brain Research Centre
- Dognosis: start-up decoding dog brain signals to detect cancer
- Investment: $6 billion public (UNESCO, 2023); $7.3 billion private (2020)
## 3. Global
| Country | Initiative |
| USA | BRAIN Initiative; Neuralink FDA approval for human trials (2024); California (2024) brain-data law |
| China | China Brain Project (2016–2030) |
| Chile | Amended Constitution for mental integrity (neurorights) |
- UNESCO prohibitions: no neural data for manipulative/political/commercial targeting; no coercive collection for employment/insurance
## 4. Applications
| Technique | Use |
| Deep Brain Stimulation (DBS) | Parkinson's, essential tremor, dystonia |
| Vagus Nerve Stimulation | Treatment-resistant depression |
| Responsive Neurostimulation | Epileptic seizures |
| Spinal Cord Stimulation | Chronic pain |
| Retinal implants | Retinitis pigmentosa |
### Does India need to upgrade biosecurity?
## 1. Concepts
- Biosecurity: prevents intentional misuse; biosafety: prevents accidental release
- Biological Weapons Convention (BWC): 1975 — first treaty banning development, production, stockpiling of bioweapons
- Ricin: toxin from castor — reported terror attempts
- ⚠ India ranks 66th on the Global Health Security Index
## 2. India's framework
| Law/Body | Role |
| Biosafety Rules, 1989 | Genetically engineered organisms |
| Recombinant DNA Research Guidelines, 2017 | Biocontainment |
| WMD Act, 2005 | Criminalises biological weapons |
| DBT, NCDC, Plant Quarantine Organisation | Lab governance, surveillance, plant health |
- India member of: BWC and Australia Group
## 3. Other countries
| Country | Measure |
| USA | National Biodefense Strategy (2022–28); 2024 Synthetic DNA Screening Guidance |
| EU | Health Security Framework (2022) |
| China | Biosecurity Law (2021) |
| Australia | Biosecurity Act (2015) |
| UK | Biological Security Strategy (2023) |
## 4. Cartagena Protocol
- Cartagena Protocol on Biosafety: under CBD; legally binding; on Living Modified Organisms
- ⚠ Adopted in Montreal on 29 January 2000 — not in Cartagena

### Sodium-ion batteries — case for India
## 1. Lithium-ion status
- Li-ion price: ~$1,100/kWh (early 2010s) → ~$108/kWh (2025)
- PLI for Advanced Chemistry Cells (2021): ~40 GWh allocated, only ~1 GWh commissioned
- Cathode: lithium metal oxides — LiCoO₂, LiMn₂O₄, LiFePO₄; ⚠ anode = graphite (graphite is not a cathode material)
## 2. Sodium-ion advantages
- Energy density approaching LFP; layered-oxide chemistries outperform polyanionic and Prussian blue analogues
- Safety: lower thermal-runaway temperature; ⚠ not "Dangerous Goods" for transport; can be stored/shipped at zero volts (Li-ion at ≤30% charge)
- Aluminium current collectors on both electrodes (Li-ion uses copper); sodium from soda ash
- Global capacity: ~70 GWh (2025) → ~400 GWh by 2030; expected to undercut Li-ion costs by 2035
### PERT — single genome-editing strategy for many disorders
## 1. Study
- Journal: Nature; by Broad Institute, Harvard, University of Minnesota
- PERT: Prime-Editing-mediated Readthrough of Premature Termination codons — edits a tRNA gene into a suppressor tRNA
- Target: nonsense mutations (premature stop codon, e.g. TAG) — ~25% of disease-causing genetic changes
## 2. Key numbers
- Human cells have 418 tRNA genes; 4 promising — leucine, arginine, tyrosine, serine
- 17,000+ configurations tested; enzyme PE6c + PE3 strategy
- Efficiency: 60–80% vs HDR 10–20%
- Mouse models: Batten, Tay-Sachs, Niemann-Pick C1; Hurler syndrome — activity restored to 1.7% of normal
### India's drone ecosystem
- Fixed-wing UAVs cannot land vertically or hover
### Lenacapavir — Zimbabwe rollout and HIV capsid science
## 1. Drug
- ⚠ First twice-yearly injectable PrEP for HIV — developed by Gilead Sciences
- US FDA approved on 18 June 2025 — world's first capsid-based HIV inhibitor; 100% effective in trials
- ⚠ Not a vaccine; only for HIV-negative persons; does not replace ART
## 2. Zimbabwe
- Rollout 19 February 2026 at Epworth (~20 km south of Harare); 46,000 doses, 24 sites
- Funded by US government and Global Fund; Zimbabwe among 9 countries chosen by WHO for early access
- Zimbabwe PrEP toolkit: oral PrEP (2016), dapivirine vaginal ring (2021), injectable cabotegravir (2024), lenacapavir (2026)
## 3. Science
- Resistant HIV replicates at only 20–30% of normal — capsid is a high-constraint target; capsid shape paper 1999 (Science)
- AZT (zidovudine), 1987 — first drug, targeted reverse transcriptase
- Global: ~41 million living with HIV; ~27 million (67%) in Sub-Saharan Africa; 44 million+ deaths since 1980s
- ⚠ Tattooing can transmit Hepatitis B and HIV — not Chikungunya
## 4. CA360 details
- Brand name "Yeztugo"; ⚠ CA360 cites ~99.9% effectiveness (daily: 100% in trials); Gilead est. 1987, Foster City

| SWaDeS | DRDO Defence Laboratory Jodhpur — portable Sea Water Desalination System |
